The "Rosicrucian Library" series was a major project by the publisher Chamuel to bring esoteric and mystical classics to a French-speaking audience during the occult revival in Paris.</meta>

# PRACTICAL THEOSOPHY
<note>original Latin title: "Theosophia Practica". <term>Theosophy|Literally "divine wisdom," referring here to a tradition of mystical insight into the nature of God and the universe, distinct from the later 19th-century Theosophical Society.</term></note>

It shows a human head in profile, facing left. Inside the head and throat area are various alchemical and planetary symbols corresponding to internal faculties. At the very top of the oval, within a radiant sun-like cloud, the Hebrew name of God, Yahweh <note>original: "יהוה", the Tetragrammaton</note>, is inscribed. The entire diagram is a mystical chart typical of Gichtel's teachings on how divine forces manifest within the human body and soul.</image-desc>

## J.-G. GICHTEL

TRITHEMIUS. — *Treatise on Secondary Causes.* <note>original: "Traité des Causes secondes." Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516) was a German Benedictine abbot and polymath. This work discusses the "Intelligences" or angels that govern the cyclical periods of world history.</note>

RABBI ISSACHAR BAER. — *Commentary on the Song*
         *of Songs.* <note>original: "Commentaire sur le Cantique des Cantiques." This likely refers to the mystical interpretations of the biblical Song of Solomon, a central text in Kabbalistic study.</note>

REVEREND FATHER ESPRIT SABBATHIER. — *The Ideal Shadow of the*
         *Universal Wisdom.* <note>original: "L'Ombre idéale de la Sagesse Universelle." R. P. stands for Révérend Père. Sabbathier was an 18th-century Capuchin monk whose work explored the bridge between divine wisdom and human understanding.</note>

->FORTHCOMING<-

MARTINEZ DE PASQUALLY. — *Treatise on the Reintegration*
         *of Beings.* <note>original: "Traité de la Réintégration des Êtres." This is the foundational text of "Martinism," written by Jacques de Livron Joachim de la Tour de la Casa Martinez de Pasqually (c. 1727–1774). It describes the spiritual fall of humanity and the path to returning to a divine state.</note>

# PREFACE

## OF THE 1736 EDITION

**1.** — The beloved reader will receive here, contrary to all expectations, what they have hoped for for such a long time. These are the illustrations of the *inner man* <term>inner man: a spiritual concept, common in 18th-century mysticism, referring to the soul or the divine spark within a person, as opposed to the physical "outer" self.</term> that the author kept hidden away for fifteen years, until the end of his life, and which we ourselves kept for another ten years.

**2.** — They should have been kept secret for much longer, if *Love* <note>original: "l’Amour"; here personified as a divine or guiding force, likely representing God's will.</note> had not concerned itself with the matter and, to please the seekers <note>original: "amateurs"; in this context, it refers to "lovers of truth" or those devoted to spiritual study rather than mere hobbyists.</note>, finally gave us permission and urged us to entrust them to the printing press.

**3.** — We rejoice in the great

<note>The text ends mid-sentence at the bottom of the page.</note>

<gloss>inner man, Love, 1736 edition, printing</gloss>

It establishes the spiritual importance of the figures and the long-awaited nature of the release.</summary>

<keywords>inner man, mysticism, 1736 edition, spiritual illustrations, secret manuscripts, divine love, printing history</keywords>

7. — By the same influx of grace, we have followed the trial of Jesus <note>original: "le procès de Jésus"; this likely refers to the "process" or imitation of the life and suffering of Christ as a mystical path</note> in spirit, and by the virtue of Him who is the beginning and the end of our faith.

<meta>This page marks the beginning of the book's narrative content, moving from the technical printer’s register to the author's personal introduction. It establishes the spiritual and philosophical framework of the work.</meta>

# INTRODUCTION

## TO THE READER, FRIEND OF GOD AND OF WISDOM

1. — Although I have hesitated long to share this small heavenly flower—won through a long and tiring struggle against this coarse and filthy world, which tramples underfoot the pearl of the knowledge of God and of Self and persecutes those who seek it—my dear colleagues have compelled me through their repeated exhortations to bring to light this representation of the Divine mystery in its innermost depths <note>original: "fond le plus intérieur." In mystical traditions, this refers to the *Ungrund* or the deepest ground of being where the soul meets the Divine.</note>, so that they may have a mirror before their eyes in which to contemplate themselves.

2. — May this serve them as a memorial and a warning; for when Adam committed his fault, he awakened and made active the cold and wrathful Darkness within his own life-forms. This provoked in all his descendants a violent struggle of evil against good—a conflict of Darkness, Wrath, and the "No" against Light, Love, and the "Yes" <note>This refers to the dialectical philosophy of Jakob Böhme, where the "Yes" and "No" represent the fundamental dualities of the divine will and existence.</note>.

<vocab>God, Wisdom, knowledge, Divine mystery, Adam, Darkness, Wrath, No, Light, Love, Yes</vocab>

<summary>The author introduces his mystical work as a hard-won "heavenly flower," explaining that he published it at the urging of friends to serve as a spiritual mirror for those seeking the knowledge of God and the self.</summary>
<keywords>mysticism, spiritual struggle, Adam, the Fall, divine mystery, dualism, wisdom, self-knowledge</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Introduction to the Reader" from page 11. It moves from the historical context of the Fall of Adam to practical advice on maintaining spiritual vigilance in the face of competing cosmic forces.</meta>

<page-num>12</page-num>

# PRACTICAL THEOSOPHY <note>original: "THEOSOPHIA PRACTICA." From the Greek *theos* (God) and *sophia* (wisdom), referring to a mystical system of direct knowledge of the Divine through internal experience.</note>

3. — **Therefore**, let everyone stand on their guard; let them pray and keep watch with diligence and severity, and exercise a vigilant supervision over all the venomous influences of the infernal and earthly constellations <note>In 17th-century mystical thought, "constellations" refers not just to stars, but to spiritual and astrological forces that exert pressure on the human soul.</note>; let them take exact care regarding the covetousness, the desire, and the imagination of their own character, so that the Good might not be overwhelmed and so that their fall and separation do not become even more pronounced.

4. — **This happens** very easily and very quickly when the soul becomes complacent and relaxes its effort a bit, when it turns thoughtlessly toward the earthly aspects of the Spirit of this world <note>original: "l'Esprit de ce monde." This likely refers to the *spiritus mundi*, the governing astral or material force of the physical universe.</note>, or when it allows an evil thought against its brother to penetrate within, and exalts itself through pride above the Thrones <note>Thrones: One of the traditional nine choirs of angels. To exalt oneself above them suggests a state of extreme spiritual pride, mirroring the fall of Lucifer.</note>.

5. — **For the three Principles** <note>Three Principles: A central concept in the philosophy of Jakob Böhme, referring to the Dark World (wrath/fire), the Light World (love/majesty), and the Outer World (nature/the physical realm).</note> hunger for the soul: each seeks dominion over it and strives until it has obtained it or has been defeated by the strongest—that is to say, until the Light, which alone is all-powerful, has bound and subdued them, as I myself have experienced during the long years of my difficult struggle.

6. — **It is not enough** to begin this undertaking with good intentions; one must not, after having worked hard at it for eight,

<vocab>venomous influences, infernal constellations, the Spirit of this world, Thrones, three Principles, Light, theosophy</vocab>

<summary>The author warns the reader to maintain strict spiritual vigilance against the "venomous" influences of the world and the pride that leads to spiritual falls. He describes the soul as a battlefield where three cosmic Principles compete for control, a struggle he has personally endured for many years.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual vigilance, constellations, Three Principles, spiritual pride, the soul, conflict, Light vs Darkness</keywords>

<page-num>13</page-num>
<header>INTRODUCTION</header>

nine, ten years or more, to foolishly weaken, embitter other weak hearts, and plunge them along with oneself into eternal misfortune.

**7.** — But one must sacrifice their whole life to the Lord, abandon themselves to Him in body, soul, and spirit, in fortune and health, and understand that it is the sole grace of God that has called us to regeneration <note>Regeneration: In this mystical context, it refers to "spiritual rebirth," a process where the inner spiritual person is born anew through divine grace.</note>; one must promise to God and his Christ an eternal fidelity and constancy, and commit for life to being a witness of the truth.

**8.** — What use can it be to love one's earthly life? It is perishable however, and, according to the word of Christ, one will lose it. It is much better not to put one's hand to the plow <note>A reference to Luke 9:62: "No one who puts his hand to the plow and looks back is fit for service in the kingdom of God."</note> and wait patiently in one's simplicity for the call of God, or—if one is worked by the Spirit of God—to humbly count one's inner strengths, so as not to oppose ten thousand soldiers to twenty thousand <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 14:31 about a king calculating his strength before going to war; here it serves as a metaphor for spiritual preparation before facing demonic or worldly temptations.</note>; it is better to go slowly than to fly to meet the enemy, despise him, and be finally overwhelmed by him.

**9** — I write to warn those who walk joyfully behind the Lord, but who have not yet fought. For usually, the beginning is sweet, joyful, and very pleasant; but when it becomes serious, that

<summary>This page warns seekers that the spiritual path requires total lifelong commitment rather than a brief burst of enthusiasm. It uses biblical metaphors of the plow and the warring king to emphasize the need for humble self-examination and endurance before facing spiritual trials.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, spiritual rebirth, perseverance, spiritual warfare, sacrifice, fidelity, hand to the plow</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Introduction to the Reader," following the warning on page 13 about the dangers of spiritual stagnation. The text picks up mid-sentence, describing the intense struggle required to move from the initial "sweetness" of the spiritual path into the deep, transformative work of the soul.</meta>

<page-num>14</page-num>
<header>THEOSOPHIA PRACTICA</header>

...the soul seeks to withdraw its will from the outer constellation <note>The "outer constellation" refers to the influence of the physical stars and the worldly, material environment on the human soul.</note> to turn toward God, in its <term>CENTER</term> <note>CENTER: In theosophical mysticism, this is the "punctum," the deepest point of the soul where the human meets the Divine.</note>, to abandon all that is visible and pass through the eighth form of Fire <note>Eighth form of Fire: In the system of Jakob Boehme and his followers, there are seven "forms" or properties of nature. The "eighth" represents the transition or breakthrough into the divine Light-world beyond the cycle of nature.</note>; this requires tireless labor, even sweats of blood; for the soul must then struggle with God and men.

**10.** — Therefore, as soon as you wish to return to [worldly] life and love it once more, the Devil comes with seven more wicked spirits <note>A reference to Matthew 12:45, describing how a person who falls back into sin is beset by even greater spiritual dangers.</note>, surrounds your poor soul in every form, and makes you spend the rest of your life in great misery, poverty, hunger, and the gluttonous and earthly cares of a slave, only to be finally cast into the fire of God with trembling, anguish, and pain. I have, alas! known true examples of such misfortunes!

**11.** — During this time, you are your own craftsman. If you remain in humility and make of yourself an angel, you will be an angel. But if you form within yourself a proud, turbulent demon, you are a demon, and God cannot forgive you.

# **CHAPTER ONE**

## **FOREWORD**

1. — Reader who seeks God, you are here fully presented with the <term>wheel of birth <gloss>original: "roue de la genèse". A term used to describe the cyclical and eternal unfolding of nature and the divine qualities.</gloss></term> according to the <term>three Principles <gloss>The three realms of existence in Boehmean thought: the source of fire/desire, the source of light/love, and the external physical world.</gloss></term>, so that you may effectively see how the forms are engendered according to their order in the <term>temperament <gloss>original: "tempérament". This refers to the state of perfect harmony or "temperature" where all conflicting spiritual qualities are balanced.</gloss></term>.

2. — Imagine this in a living way within your soul; you will then understand the following figures <note>In this context, "figures" likely refers to the symbolic illustrations or diagrams that accompany the text to explain spiritual concepts.</note> of our Author with much greater ease, for true understanding is internal.

3. — This is why he wishes only to show, as an introduction, what the first-created <term>image of God <gloss>The "Imago Dei," or the original spiritual state of humanity before the biblical Fall.</gloss></term> was before the <term>fall <gloss>The loss of the original divine state and the descent into a world of conflict and mortality.</gloss></term>, a reality that the author recognized practically in the <term>hidden depths of the spirit <gloss>original: "l'occulte de l'esprit". This refers to mystical or intuitive knowledge gained through direct spiritual experience rather than academic study.</gloss></term>, and which he has unfolded for you in the figures of the perfect man.

4. — And, if you follow the author with diligence, <term>regeneration <gloss>The spiritual process of being "born again" into the light of God, restoring the soul to its original divine image.</gloss></term> will be realized for you, and you will achieve it.

<vocab>wheel of birth, three Principles, temperament, image of God, the fall, hidden depths of the spirit, the perfect man, regeneration</vocab>

## OF THE GREAT MYSTERY
# OF DIVINE REVELATION
### ACCORDING TO TIME AND ETERNITY
### IN THE HUMAN FORM

1. — If we wish to contemplate and observe man in his profound <term>internal generation <gloss>original: "génération intérieure." This refers to the spiritual process of the soul's formation and its inner development.</gloss></term>, we must, with our soul, emerge from the **ELEMENTAL** life <note>The "elemental life" refers to existence governed by the four material elements: earth, air, fire, and water.</note> and from <term>earthly siderality <gloss>original: "sidéralité terrestre." In theosophical thought, this refers to the influence of the stars and the "astrum" upon the human mind and physical nature.</gloss></term> and turn toward the internal divine life of Jesus Christ; we must call upon the grace of this <note>The "dear physician" is a traditional mystical title for Christ, reflecting his role in healing the "sickness" of the fallen human soul.</note> dear physician, so that he might deign to open our eyes, which have been closed until now by the devil since Paradise; so that we may recover our eye of light to recognize and contemplate God within us; otherwise everything will remain a sealed and inconceivable **MYSTERY** to our rational <term>sidereal-eye <gloss>original: "œil-sidérique." This is the intellect or natural perception that belongs to the physical universe; it is capable of logic but blind to spiritual reality.</gloss></term>.

### PRACTICAL THEOSOPHY

5. — Until merciful God finally appeared to me inwardly, face to face; until he had opened his heaven within me, and had spoken with my soul, mouth to mouth, according to the <term>Being <gloss>original Latin: "ENS." 6. — This is what he has faithfully accomplished: To Him, then, be the honor, the power, the strength, the kingdom, and the splendor throughout Eternity. Amen!

8. — The first constellation is the most external; it is the ruler of the <term>natural man <gloss>original: "homme naturel." In this context, it refers to the earthly, unregenerate human driven by physical and social instincts rather than divine ones.</gloss></term>, which carries him toward various arts, trades, studies, and business affairs, making individuals good or bad, rich or poor, high or low, as anyone can observe. Referring to the Book of Revelation, Chapter 5, often interpreted by mystics as describing the opening of the soul's inner seals.</gloss></term>.

# THEOSOPHIA PRACTICA <note>Latin for "Practical Wisdom."</note>

15. — The hidden <term>Dark-World</term> <note>In this mystical system, the "Dark-World" represents the first stage of existence, characterized by contraction and longing before the light of God's love is introduced.</note> contains the first three <term>forms</term> <note>referring to the "Seven Properties of Nature" described by the mystic Jacob Böhme; the first three are generally contraction, friction, and anguish.</note> up to the Fire, and is called Hell, apart from the Fire; it is the severe power of God, toward which the Devil reached, and which he turned into hell, as did Adam;

# CHAPTER TWO

## FOREWORD

1. — In the figure shown opposite, which is the first from our **CENTRAL** <note>This likely refers to Jacob Böhme (1575–1624), the German mystic whose "central" or fundamental insights inspired this work.</note> and enlightened author, the dear reader learns what the fall of Adam is and how he broke away entirely from God and the "Yes" <note>In this mystical tradition, the "Yes" (the *Ija*) represents the divine unity and affirmation, while the "No" represents the division and conflict of the material world.</note>.

2. — He has become a personal ray, of a foreign and false **ESSENCE**, which is called "the lie." (This is Satan, the ancient serpent, reared up in the heart of our first parents, and whom, accordingly, our adorable Savior—who is spirit and life to us—calls the father of lies <note>original: "Ioh 8, 44." / John 8:44: "When he lies, he speaks his native language, for he is a liar and the father of lies."</note>). This essence turns upon itself, in both good and evil, and reaches only the outer sun <note>The "outer sun" refers to the physical world and temporal light, as opposed to the inner, divine light of the spiritual sun.</note>.

3. — The soul is dead within; it has become the hell where eternal corruption is at work.

4. — Let man then distance himself far from this and turn his courage <note>In this context, "courage" implies the "heart's resolve" or the fundamental orientation of the will.</note> toward God.

5. — This is what is called doing penance; the operation takes place in the heart. Prayers push the heart with strength out of the Infernal Abyss, knocking with violence at the door of Heaven and drawing Heaven to the heart through Faith.

6. — This comprises <term>Regeneration</term> <note>The spiritual "re-birthing" of the soul into its original divine state, a core concept in Böhme’s theology.</note>; the spark of Faith—

<summary>This chapter introduces the first symbolic illustration, explaining how Adam's fall transformed the soul into a "lie" or a "hell" and detailing the necessity of repentance to begin the process of Regeneration.</summary>

### CHAPTER TWO

[eter]nal image of God according to the worlds of darkness and light.

2. — And just as these Three who are but One <note>A reference to the Trinity, reflecting the mystical belief that the structure of the Godhead is mirrored in the structure of the human soul.</note> distinguish themselves in their ESSENCES and their operations, so too is the triple life of man, and it cannot be conceived otherwise: each remains within its own will, its fire, or its spirit.

3. — Each fire <note>In this tradition, "fire" represents a fundamental spiritual drive or life-force. There is a "dark fire" of desire and a "light fire" of divine love.</note> also has its own CENTER and covets the specific nourishment that is pleasing to it; and it takes no other, which is why we find different ways of living among men.

**54.** — If this GLOBE were ignited by the fire of divine Love, and if it produced a clear light in the deepest part of the heart, the old serpent, Satan, would be cast down into the Darkness.

**55.** — And you would be a regenerated angelic man, a child of Light who would fight against flesh and blood, the Devil, and the world; you would crucify your own self-will with all its works and follow Christ in his regeneration <note>Regeneration refers to the "new birth," a central theme in this text where the human soul is restored to its original, divine state.</note>.

<vocab>SPIRIT OF THE WORLD, GLOBE, Light, Devil, Fire, Wrath of God, Divine Love, Satan, Darkness, Christ, angelic man, regeneration</vocab>

**56.** — But currently you are in a form of **Antichrist** and you hate the **Light** because your works are evil. It is your own <term>irascible, creaturely Will</term> <note>The "creaturely will" refers to the ego or self-centered desires of a created being, which, when "irascible" (driven by anger or harshness), separates the soul from God.</note> that makes you perverse and damnable, and not God; it is this will that makes you walk and act according to its own laws, which makes you disobedient to the **Spirit of God** and prevents you from being the fiancé of the celestial <term>SOPHIA</term> <note>original: "SOPHIA." In this tradition, Sophia represents Divine Wisdom, often personified as a pure, heavenly maiden who acts as the spiritual bride to the regenerated soul.</note>;

**57.** — Instead, this will makes of you a personal <term>humanimal</term> <note>A portmanteau of "human" and "animal," used to describe a person who lives only to satisfy their physical, animalistic instincts rather than their spiritual potential.</note>, who lives according to your pleasure and the comforts of the external fleshly life, without a **cross** <note>The "cross" here refers to spiritual trials or the "mortification" of the ego—the necessary struggle to overcome one's lower nature.</note> or opposition, who does not call upon **Christ**, who feels neither hunger nor thirst for the heavenly presence, and who constantly strives after honors and fortune.

**58.** — *Yes*, replies the **creaturely Will**; *I can do nothing without Christ, I can alienate nothing by myself, nor give anything to God unless He allows me; if He wishes to lead me into the battle against myself, let Him arm me with the **Spirit of prayer**, so that I may persevere.*

<summary>This page critiques the "creaturely will" as the source of human perversity and spiritual blindness, contrasting the base "humanimal" life with the pursuit of Divine Wisdom (Sophia). <header>PRACTICAL THEOSOPHY <note>original: "THEOSOPHIA PRACTICA." Theosophy refers to the pursuit of direct, divine knowledge of God and the nature of the universe.</note></header>

the one who gives, and he will even return a cup of cold water.

**93.** — Christ, in speaking of his future judgment (Matthew 25) <note>original: "Matth. 25"</note> also teaches us that he will place the poor at his right and the rich at his left, saying: I was hungry, thirsty, naked, sick and a prisoner, and what you have done or not done to my poor members <note>In this context, "members" refers to the "members of the Body of Christ," meaning fellow human beings—particularly those in need.</note>, you have done to me, or you have not done; and he will give to each a reward according to their works.

**94.** — To this, reason <note>The author uses "reason" here in a pejorative sense to mean "worldly logic" or "human calculation" which lacks faith.</note> replies: If the rich were required to support the poor, they would fall into misery themselves and would no longer have anything to share. To this Tauler <note>Johannes Tauler (c. 1300–1361) was a famous German Dominican mystic and preacher known for emphasizing the inner spiritual life and detachment from worldly things.</note> responds that if the rich reached such a height, they would be in the poverty of Christ, who would occupy himself with them and provide for their needs.

### PRACTICAL THEOSOPHY

145. — Let us return then and call upon Jesus, who descended from His heavenly Father to help us, to withdraw our creaturely will <note>“Creaturely will” (volonté créaturelle) is a term used in mystical theology to describe the individual, self-centered will of a created being which has become separated from the divine will.</note> from every earthly desire and turn it toward His Father—and let us be submissive to Him even unto death on the Cross.

147. — The early Christians **PRACTICED** this commandment and thus bore witness that they loved Christ and kept His law.

148. — From this flows all His teaching: “Blessed are the poor in spirit, for the kingdom of heaven belongs to them.” “No one can serve two masters at once. Look at the birds of the air: they neither sow nor reap, yet your heavenly Father feeds them.”

### CHAPTER THREE

...[there is] no need for a new birth <note>original: "nouvel accouchement." In this mystical context, the author is clarifying that "Regeneration" is not a second physical birth, but a spiritual one.</note>, but simply for a renewal and a conversion from the exterior to the interior, so that there may be RENOVATION through pure divinity.

**26.** — The <term>old carcass</term> <note>original: "vieille carcasse." This refers to the physical body or the "old Adam"—the unrefined human nature that remains subject to decay and earthly needs even after spiritual awakening.</note> is and remains fragile; and through its perpetual hunger, it causes as much trouble as the manure spread upon a field to make the wheat grow.

**27.** — It exhausts all the <term>provisions of the soul</term> <note>original: "munitions de l'âme." These represent the spiritual resources or internal strengths the soul uses to sustain itself.</note>, bringing it poverty, anguish, pain, and sorrow, so that the soul can no longer find joy or rest in the outward, pleasant life; it becomes sad and grieves over the hardships of earthly life and the <term>diversity of things</term> <note>original: "variété des choses." In theosophical thought, this refers to the distracting multiplicity of the material world which pulls the soul away from the singular "Unity" of God.</note>, and begins to think of its <note>A reference to the biblical parable of the Prodigal Son, who remembers the comfort of his father's home only after hitting rock bottom in the world.</note> Father’s house.

**28.** — And if this "bearer of anguish" were not there, the wonders of the wisdom of God would not be seen, and the prayers of the faithful could not rise up with such ardor and fervor.

### CHAPTER THREE 101

46. — [knowledge of my] divine birth, and moved to punish my dissolute life, I was led to show myself to the priests, who rang the alarm bell <note>original: "sonnèrent le tocsin," a signal of danger or public call to arms</note> and denounced me to the authorities as an <term>enthusiast</term> <note>A 17th-century derogatory term for those claiming direct personal inspiration from God, viewed as a threat to organized religion</note>, a lunatic, a heretic, and an <term>anabaptist</term> <note>A radical religious movement often persecuted for its rejection of infant baptism and state-church authority</note>.

47. — They showered me with mockery, insults, and shame; having me dragged through the streets by the sergeants and wanting to have me beheaded; but, as they could not come to an agreement, they abandoned everything at once and banished me from the city for life.

48. — When I was locked in a foul dungeon, the Devil attempted his first assault, and **TEMPTED** me with such terrible doubts that I was on the point of taking my knife to deliver my miserable life from this pain.

49. — This struggle was so fierce and so violent that the skin of my knees was torn off by dragging myself on the floor, without my noticing it because of my inner anguish; for the Devil put doubt into all my prayers.

# CHAPTER FOUR

## FOREWORD

1. — Here come the third and fourth **FIGURES** <note>In this context, *figures* refers to the complex symbolic diagrams or engravings used in theosophical texts to illustrate spiritual concepts.</note> of the author, which have been placed at the beginning of the book because they represent the first image of God in perfect regeneration through Christ. They are shown with the **PECTORAL** <note>The *pectoral* refers to the breastplate worn by the High Priest in the Old Testament, signifying spiritual authority and judgment.</note> of the two eternal **PRINCIPLES**, the *Urim* and the *Thummim* <note>original: "Urim" and "Thummim"; Hebrew terms traditionally translated as "Lights" and "Perfections," used as oracular tools on the High Priest's breastplate.</note>, representing the light and the perfection of **the Spirit**. They also feature the **HUMERAL** <note>The *humeral* or *ephod* is a shoulder-garment; here it symbolizes the "government" or "burden" placed upon the shoulders of the savior.</note> and the royal mantle of the kingdom of **JESUS** over the external **PRINCIPLE**; read **Isaiah**, **IX**; 6 <note>"For unto us a child is born... and the government shall be upon his shoulder."</note>.

2. — The reader can see, from the very beginning, what the **AUTHOR** intends throughout the entire work: to describe outwardly in Jesus this image which is found in each of us, according to **Saint Paul** <note>This refers to the Pauline doctrine of the "New Man" or the "Christ within."</note>.

3. — This is the original divine order according to which the **CENTRES** <note>In mystical philosophy, *centres* are spiritual focal points or "wheels" of energy within the human soul.</note> are arranged in the new man; the first and second **PRINCIPLES** in the front; the third behind in the temperament.

4. — All three have their origin in the heart, and plunge into one another for the baptism of the **Holy Spirit**, so that the world of Light manifests above, and that of Fire below.

<summary>This page introduces the symbolic diagrams of the "New Man," using the vestments of the biblical High Priest to explain how divine principles are organized within the human soul. It emphasizes that the spiritual structure seen in Jesus is a template for the inner transformation of every person.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, spiritual anatomy, High Priest vestments, Urim and Thummim, Holy Spirit, mysticism, Isaiah 9:6, divine principles</keywords>

5. — The world of Air, depicted along the spine, is the product of the first two <note>Referring to the first two "Principles" of divine manifestation—the dark fire-world and the light-world.</note>; it receives their powers into its union, and it shines when the King appears in his magnificence; it is then like the radiance of a multitude of gemstones; this is what is called the **Heavenly Jerusalem** (*Revelation* 21). All the forces act in concert and cause their virtues and their light to play through one another, like a **Sea of Crystal**, of which the **AUTHOR** <note>Likely referring to Jacob Boehme, the primary source for these theosophical concepts.</note> has already made mention.

6. — When Jesus begins to do battle within the soul, the majestic light withdraws and, in its place, Jesus girds us with the **ESSENCE** of Truth; he takes up the **Breastplate** <note>original: "Ecrevisse"; literally "crayfish," but in 17th-century French, this referred to a "cuirass à l'écrevisse," a type of armor made of overlapping plates. It is a mystical interpretation of the "Breastplate of Righteousness."</note> of Justice, which is the daily **EQUIPMENT** <note>original: "MONTURE"; literally "mount" or "setting," but here referring to the spiritual "outfitting" or "harness" of the believer.</note> of the true soldier of Christ; he presents himself before God with the shield of faith in the hand of the will: such is our adornment according to Christ.

7. — The **Virgin** <note>In this tradition, the "Virgin" represents Sophia or Divine Wisdom, the mirror of God and the spiritual spouse of the soul.</note> keeps the wedding garment at home; she covers herself with the **PRINCIPLE** <note>A "Principle" is a fundamental realm of existence; theosophy typically describes three: the dark/fire, the light/love, and the external/material.</note> at the same time as the dark side of the mirror of Light over which the Holy Spirit hovers. As God has his holy bridal bed at the **CENTER** of each **PRINCIPLE** (which has become Jesus within each of those men sanctified in body, soul, and spirit), the powers flow from the head into all the members that God assembles with care, as if into the **ESSENCE** of Truth.

8. — Finally, the reader sees which celestial and infernal Forms oppose one another in the highest and deepest combat and how they lay siege to one another simultaneously.

9. — The war is all around the kingdom of the third **PRINCIPLE** <note>The "Third Principle" is the physical, visible world we inhabit.</note> which our Lord and Savior (who sup- <note>The text cuts off mid-word, likely "supports" or "supplies."</note>

It uses the metaphor of a soldier of Christ to explain how divine essences and the "Celestial Virgin" (Wisdom) interact within the believer during spiritual warfare.</summary>

### CHAPTER FOUR

(who upholds all things by the word of His power <note>original: "porte tout par les paroles de Sa puissance"; a reference to Hebrews 1:3</note>) has taken upon his shoulder and rescued from Satan’s grip within humanity.

10. — This one [Satan] does not want to abandon his rights, and <note>Likely "hurls" (original: *lance*), but the first letter is missing in this scan.</note> spews in torrents all his infernal venoms (which are the **COAGULATED Spirits** <note>In this mystical tradition, "coagulation" refers to spiritual forces hardening or manifesting into a dense, darker, or more material state.</note> of the Abyss) against the **Virginal Temperament**,

11. — Which sets up **Love**, who parries the poisoned arrows of Satan with the shield of faith in Christ and sends them back to his adversary.

12. — This combat lasts until Satan has exhausted all his anger and Jesus, prince of **Light** and **Love**, has struck him down and expelled him.

13. — Thus, our faith becomes increasingly deep, and is sublimated like a light: such is the victory by which we **SUCCESSIVELY** conquer Satan in all the **PRINCIPLES** <term>Principles: In the system of Jacob Boehme, these are the three fundamental realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light-world, and the outer material world.</term> and we finally cast him into **Hell**.

14. — Then all the evil spirits submit to the scepter of the **Love** of Jesus and bow the knee before Him.

15. — **Love**, which has suffered everything, holds Satan and the entire army of the Hells prisoner, and presses them so tightly that the evil spirits coagulate like a sea, between this world and the kingdom of Hell, and pay homage to Jesus.

16. — Our prince triumphs (as He did for the first and second **PRINCIPLES**) and leads us through the immense ocean of infernal power.

17. — The evil spirits make way and line up on both sides of the shores and cannot make a single movement without the permission of Jesus.

18. — Below is the divine **MAGICAL** eye <term>MAGICAL eye: Also known as the "Eye of Wonder," this refers to the divine capacity for insight and creation within the soul, reflecting the vision of God.</term> of Wonder and the Fire (1) which is, among the regenerated <term>Regenerated: Individuals who have experienced a spiritual rebirth, shifting their inner nature from a self-centered state to one aligned with the divine.</term>, the place where the Father produces His Son. In others, it is the Fire of divine Wrath <note>In the mystical system of Jacob Boehme, God’s nature is experienced as either a comforting Light or a consuming Fire (Wrath) depending on the spiritual state of the person.</note>.

19. — It is the foundation of heaven, of Hell <note>original: "Enfers"</note>, and of the visible world, from which are born good and evil, like light and darkness, life and death, bliss and damnation.

20. — It is not without reason that it is called **MYSTÉRIUM MAGNUM** <note>original: "MYSTÉRIUM MAGNUM," Latin for "The Great Mystery." In Boehme’s thought, this is the chaotic or "undifferentiated" source from which the entire universe and all spiritual realms emerge.</note>, for it contains two beings and two wills: Unity and Multiplicity, the latter of which introduces itself through a craving reaching toward the Fire and the Light; within the Fire is contained the natural Life, and within the Light, the holy spiritual Life of unity.

21. — It also produces two wills: one fiery, exalted, proud, and diabolical; the other humble, low, and angelic; hence the choice of the Elect <note>original: "Élus." Here, the "Elect" are those who have chosen the humble, angelic path over the proud, fiery one.</note>.

**41.** — No man can see any further into it than if he were in his mother’s womb; each **PRINCIPLE** <note>original: "PRINCIPE". # CHAPTER FIVE

->## ON THE BATTLE OF MICHAEL AND THE DRAGON<-

1. — This spiritual battle between **Love** and **Wrath** <note>original: "la Colère." In theosophical tradition, particularly that of Jakob Böhme, "Wrath" or "Fury" refers to the harsh, dark, and contractive quality of the first principles of nature when they are not tempered by Divine Light.</note>, **Light** and **Darkness**, the **Yes** and the **No**, arose in heaven within the creaturely <term>self-will<note>original: "volonté propre." 2. — Lucifer detached himself from his origin, **Eternal Love**, through a self-determined will; he inserted himself into a specific operation and into forms <note>This likely refers to the "forms of nature" or the energetic qualities that make up existence, which Lucifer attempted to dominate for his own glory.</note>, and fought against God and His Son, as well as against his brothers, the good **Angels**, who defeated him.

# CHAPTER SIX

## ON PRAYER

1. — God has revealed to me in the light of <term>Grace and Nature</term> <note>The "Light of Nature" refers to knowledge gained through the physical world and reason, while "Grace" refers to divine revelation.</note>, and I have learned through my own experience, that a pious Christian cannot remain in this world without facing adversity.

2. — Not only is he overwhelmed from without by the incredible ruses and snares of the demon; but he carries within himself his own fierce enemy, nourishing it and allowing it to grow, thereby exposing himself to great dangers.

3. — For the Devil, the World, the Wrath of God, and his own Flesh drag him toward the deep abyss of Darkness, seeking to extinguish or swallow up the small divine Light that has been kindled within him.

<vocab>Prayer, Grace, Nature, Devil, Wrath of God, Darkness, Divine Light</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces a chapter on prayer, framing it as a necessary tool for the Christian to survive the external temptations of the devil and the internal corruption of the flesh. The author emphasizes that spiritual life is a constant struggle to keep a "small divine light" from being extinguished.</summary>
<keywords>prayer, spiritual warfare, grace, nature, temptation, divine light, internal enemy</keywords>

--- Page 199 ---

->PRACTICAL THEOSOPHY<-
<note>original: "Theosophia Practica" — This refers to the practical application of "theosophy" or divine wisdom in one's daily life.</note>

...in his heart, and of which the Devil is the sworn enemy.

4. — I could do nothing better than to continually cast my own will into the most dear will of God, and to hold fast unshakeably, with supplications and sighs, to the governance of His Holy Spirit.

5. — I undertook nothing without prayer and the assent of my dear Guide <note>This likely refers to the Holy Spirit or an inner divine direction leading the author.</note>, because I was following a path completely unknown and was living in Christ, which is contrary and inconceivable to all <term>reason</term> <note>In mystical writings, human reason is often contrasted with divine revelation, the former being seen as limited or even blind to spiritual truths.</note>.

6. — I had many shocks to endure from <term>natural men</term> <note>Those who live according to the physical senses and worldly logic, lacking spiritual rebirth.</note>; friends and enemies alike rose up against me, regarding me as a strange innovator who wished to introduce a foreign teaching and method that were entirely impracticable for human Nature; they looked upon me with an evil eye, hated me, and abandoned me; and I saw that my forebodings had not deceived me.

<summary>The author describes their total surrender to God's will and the guidance of the Holy Spirit as the only way to navigate a spiritual path that defies human reason. ### CHAPTER SIX

7. — And as I resolved, in order to avoid the storm <note>The "storm" likely refers to the public controversy and religious persecution mentioned at the end of the previous chapter.</note>, to bury myself in silence, God **DIRECTED** my conduct otherwise, and brought me back to the struggle.

8. — I saw that my will was fighting against God's, and as He became too strong, I had to surrender myself entirely to Him and let Him act, even though my <term>reason</term> <note>In this mystical context, "reason" refers to limited human logic which often conflicts with divine "wisdom" or "theosophy."</note> could not foresee what He was aiming for,

9. — Until at last, through His compassion, I reached a safe harbor; my soul has found rest there, and those who provoked my pains and my doubts can no longer reach me there.

<image-desc>A small decorative printer's mark consisting of a central point with stylized rays or petals radiating outwards and downwards.</image-desc>

<term>Colophon</term> <note>From the Greek word for "finishing touch," a colophon is a brief statement at the end of a book containing information about its publication, such as the printer's name and the date of completion. In the late 19th century, these were often stylized to reflect the quality of the craftsmanship.</note>

<summary>This final page constitutes the colophon of the work, recording that the printing was completed on December 30, 1897, by the Imprimerie Professionnelle in Beauvais, France.</summary>
<keywords>colophon, printing history, Beauvais, 19th-century publication, printing house, Year of Grace</keywords>

# IN THE SAME COLLECTION

**Trithemius.** <note>Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516) was a German Benedictine abbot and a polymath known for his works on cryptography, history, and the occult.</note> — *Treatise on Secondary Causes*. <note>original: "Traité des Causes secondes"; this work, also known as *De Septem Secundeis*, discusses the seven angels who govern the cycles of world history.</note> Price: 5 francs.

**Rabbi Issachar Baer.** <note>Issachar Baer ben Marcaria was a 16th-century Jewish scholar and kabbalist.</note> — *Commentary on the Song of Solomon*. <note>original: "Cantique des Cantiques"; also known as the *Song of Songs*, this biblical book is often interpreted in mystical traditions as an allegory of the soul's union with the Divine.</note> . . . . . . . . 2 " <note>The ditto marks (") indicate the price is 2 francs.</note>

**Reverend Father Esprit Sabbathier.** <note>A 17th-century French Minim friar whose works explored the "Universal Wisdom" through a blend of Christian mysticism and Hermetic philosophy.</note> — *The Ideal Shadow of Universal Wisdom*. <note>original: "L'Ombre idéale de la Sagesse universelle."</note>

## FORTHCOMING

**Martinez de Pasqually.** <note>Jacques de Livron Joachim de la Tour de la Casa Martinez de Pasqually (c. 1727–1774) was a prominent theurgist and the founder of the "Order of Knight-Masons Elect Priests of the Universe."</note> — *Treatise on the Reintegration of Beings*. <note>original: "Traité de la Réintégration des Etres"; the fundamental text of Martinezism, describing the spiritual origin, fall, and eventual restoration of humanity to its divine state.</note>

Each chart contains numbered blocks of varying resolution patterns (lines of different thicknesses and orientations) to ensure the quality and clarity of the microfilming process. <vocab>
<term>Bibliothèque Nationale</term> (National Library): The main state library of France, located in Paris, which serves as a repository for all publications in the country.
<term>Service des nouveaux supports</term> (New Media Service): The department responsible for managing non-traditional library materials, such as microfilm, microfiche, and audio-visual records.
<term>Micrographier</term> (Microfilming): The process of photographing documents at a greatly reduced scale onto film for preservation and space-saving.
</vocab>

<summary>This page is a technical record documenting that the preceding manuscript was microfilmed for preservation by the National Library of France in July 1977. It includes resolution test targets used to ensure the photographic quality of the reproduction.</summary>
